OnCo · weekly issue

OnCo weekly, 2026-11-17

1519 changelog entries, 5 regulatory events, 2 upcoming dates, 585 corrections. Generated from the corpus; every item links to its page and, through it, to the primary source. Not medical advice.

What changed this week

New pages, new data and fixes, from the changelog.

  • 17 November 2026: Second accuracy pass on live country pages: corrections to 53 pages after every claim was read against its source again, including what the IAEA radiotherapy directory lists for 36 countries, WHO's HPV vaccination data where pages had said there was none, and sources pages had called unreadable that do answer
  • 17 November 2026: The first weekly gap fill read from FDA sources lands, each fact checked against the FDA list and device records: the Guardant360 Liquid CDx blood test approved on 19 May 2026 with its seven drug pairings, and four companion tests linked to the biomarkers they read
  • 17 November 2026: More of the graph is joined up: 1,251 confirmed links between people and the trials whose papers they wrote, matched by ORCID or full name and affiliation; who pays for 21 tests in the US and UK, from Medicare national coverage decisions and NICE guidance; fifty warning-card lines now name the protocol that manages them; and eight wrong side-effect links are removed
  • 17 November 2026: Twenty-two more care protocols close the gaps in the census, among them stoma care, drain care at home, transplant conditioning, veno-occlusive disease, lung biopsy, intrathecal chemotherapy in children, end-of-life care for children, advance care planning and stopping smoking after a diagnosis; 212 protocols in all, with a table of what is covered and what is still open
  • 17 November 2026: Shasqi in depth: its clinical programme SQ3370, its trial and the click-activated chemistry behind it, from the company's own pages, the registry and the published papers; the weekly gap-fill job gets the tools it needs to read its allowed sources; and the command-line tool and MCP server are rebuilt to know about care protocols
  • 17 November 2026: A contributor's education work arrives, 215 new records on biomarkers, immune tests, specimens, RNA and clinical evidence, with every source checked; and a backlog of corrections lands: new FDA approvals including pimicotinib, four generic icons removed from logos, mislabelled trials fixed, radiotherapy counts for Madagascar and Angola, the two Cuban lung cancer vaccines, and 33 records renamed for their subject

Regulatory events, past 7 days

Dated filings, approvals, letters and label changes recorded on product pages.

  • 2026-11-16 · Giredestrant · approval (US): FDA approved giredestrant (Wilserdly, Genentech, Inc.), an oral estrogen receptor antagonist, in combination with everolimus for the treatment of adults with estrogen receptor (ER)-positive, human epidermal growth factor receptor 2 (HER2)-negative, estrogen receptor 1 ( ESR1) -mutated locally advanced or metastatic breast cancer, as detected by an FDA-authorized test, whose disease has recurred during or after completion of endocrine-based adjuvant therapy, or has progressed on at least one endocrine-based regimen in the metastatic setting. source
  • 2026-11-15 · Ivonescimab · crl (US): Complete response letter, announced by Summit on 15 November 2026 (the letter's own date is not stated): "The CRL indicates that the BLA cannot be approved in its present form, as the FDA determined that substantial evidence of effectiveness is not established in the absence of a statistically significant overall survival (OS) improvement." source
  • 2026-11-14 · Ivonescimab · pdufa (US): PDUFA target date source
  • 2026-11-13 · Durvalumab · approval (US): FDA approved durvalumab (Imfinzi, AstraZeneca) in combination with enfortumab vedotin-ejfv as neoadjuvant treatment, followed by single agent durvalumab as adjuvant treatment after radical cystectomy, for the treatment of adult patients with muscle invasive bladder cancer (MIBC) who are ineligible for or who have refused cisplatin based chemotherapy. source
  • 2026-11-10 · Pimicotinib · approval (US): FDA approved pimicotinib (Pimjetmi, EMD Serono, Inc.), a kinase inhibitor, for adults with symptomatic tenosynovial giant cell tumor (TGCT) for which surgical resection may cause worsening functional limitation or severe morbidity. The notice adds: "Pimicotinib received fast track and breakthrough therapy designation." source

  • 2026-11-12 · Intention to treat: The account overstated real-world representativeness, defined modified and per-protocol sets too narrowly, treated evaluable analyses as necessarily inflated and asserted a site-wide default without verification (ICH E9 analysis-set and safety sections and the E9(R1) estimand framework) · Preserves assignment while qualifying exclusions, missing outcomes, design and sensitivity analysis; withdraws the unsupported site-wide default and uses an explicit fictional denominator example
  • 2026-11-12 · Endpoint: Meeting a primary endpoint was defined only as beating a comparator, and secondary outcomes were categorically described as not powered (ICH trial-design guidance, CONSORT reporting and FDA's December 2018 endpoint guidance) · Refers to the actual planned question and testing rule; separates event definitions, clocks, composite components and context-dependent surrogate evidence
  • 2026-11-12 · Data maturity: Unreached medians were conflated with unreliable treatment comparisons, censoring was reduced to event-free cutoff observations and regulatory follow-up requirements were generalised (Primary KEYNOTE-522 abstracts, registry analyses, NIST methods and ICH guidance) · Distinguishes median outcome, follow-up, event information and precision; removes the universal regulatory assertion and retains the named trial's planned analyses
  • 2026-11-12 · KEYNOTE-522: The lead made an unsupported survival superlative; response cohorts and dates were compressed, and exploratory residual-disease strata were described without their uncertainty (Primary reports, the label, sponsor-posted registry and the authors' 2026 conference abstract; Pusztai's 2024 exploratory abstract) · States the whole assigned strategy, separates first-602, first-1,002 and all-1,174 response analyses, keeps each survival cutoff and preserves the seven-year figures with a primary conference citation; qualifies post-treatment subgroup estimates
  • 2026-11-12 · OptimICE-pCR: The summary described a further year of postoperative immunotherapy and implied that pCR meant all cancer had disappeared (The current registry specifies the postoperative randomisation, response requirement and 27-week comparison) · States the actual continuation-versus-observation question, non-inferiority endpoint, duration and recruiting state without presenting safe omission as established
  • 2026-11-12 · Trial protocol: A categorical sentence treated lack of a traceable prespecified analysis as enough to classify every result as merely a hypothesis (ICH and CONSORT distinguish evidence role, planning, relevant outcome access and reporting) · Requests the dated plan and access history, distinguishing confirmatory questions, planned exploration and later discoveries rather than classifying a result from its p-value alone
  • 2026-11-12 · Analytical and clinical validation: The lead limited clinical validity to predicting a patient outcome, while the summary implied one universal validation ladder (MedlinePlus Genetics separates detecting a variant, its relationship to disease presence or risk, and useful clinical information) · States three intended-use questions and distinguishes reliable measurement, clinical meaning and consequences of a decision
  • 2026-11-12 · External validation: The lead defined external validation through a different hospital, country or platform and called it the only evidence that a result travels; the summary favoured other investigators/platforms and dismissed internal evidence as no result (TRIPOD-Cluster explanation, Box 1, distinguishes apparent, internal and external evaluation and includes later-period data from the same investigators) · States the evaluation question and its limits, preserves the value of internal resampling and removes a universal pass/fail implication
  • 2026-11-12 · External validation, retained benchmark assertions: Routine TCGA-to-METABRIC testing and a typical concordance-index decline were asserted without primary verification here (Source and field comparison) · Intentionally withdraws those generalisations rather than calling them false; retains the separately sourced Michiels seven-study result at its historical scope
  • 2026-11-12 · mRNA manufacturing: The lead, process description and cards generalised modified nucleotides, a cell-free whole chain, production in weeks, process invariance and fixed storage temperatures (TNBC-MERIT primary methods specify ordinary uridine and report a 34 to 125 day sample-to-release range; Rojas reports another uridine lipoplex, with product-specific formulation and protocol) · Replaces generalisations with a specified product, manufacturing clock and separate quality/handling evidence; preserves the earlier scoped mechanism paragraph and source links
  • 2026-11-12 · mRNA manufacturing, retained commercial narrative: Facility locations, supply/patent rankings and an industrialisation prediction had no primary verification in this assessment (Field-by-field comparison with the cited study and manufacturing evidence) · Deliberately withdraws these assertions pending named source follow-up, without calling them false; the lipid-nanoparticle owner retains formulation teaching
  • 2026-11-11 · Agretopicity: The lead and opening defined agretopicity itself as the mutant-versus-normal comparison (Duan's original definition and Ghorani's distinct predicted-affinity calculation, checked in their primary text) · Distinguishes peptide-MHC interaction from the differential index, retains both formula conventions and the scoped model/cohort findings, and clarifies Simple English
  • 2026-11-11 · Neoantigen: Novel tumour sequence was said to guarantee an immune target that could be attacked without harming normal cells (TESLA primary prediction/validation methods and the separate presentation, recognition and safety questions) · Removes the blanket safety claim, adds a candidate-evidence framework, withdraws eight obsolete unreviewed translations and corrects Simple English
  • 2026-11-11 · Cross-presentation: The opening confused tumour-cell class-I display with naive CD8 priming and described a single intracellular route as universal (Cross-presentation review abstract, RNA-delivery study and T-cell activation teaching) · Separates display from priming and retains the previously scoped model findings and direct-presentation explanation
  • 2026-11-11 · Exome and genome sequencing: The lead implied perfect coverage of all genes or all DNA, with a universal depth/pairing rule and unqualified bias/depth comparisons (MedlinePlus definitions and Jones's targeted/exome methods) · Names the intended territory and workflow limits, removes universal depth/pairing claims, withdraws eight obsolete unreviewed translations and corrects Simple English

  • 2026-11-11 · Placebo: The lead required every cancer-trial placebo to accompany standard treatment, while the summary ruled out blinding whenever adverse effects reveal treatment (Complete text of FDA's August 2019 cancer-trial placebo and blinding guidance, checked with NCI's trial explanation) · Retains selected justified placebo-only settings and standard-care add-on designs; describes possible loss of blinding and qualified unblinding recommendations; corrects Simple and withdraws eight stale unreviewed machine summaries

The 9% is WHO's programme coverage of that year's target group for the last dose, not the share of girls vaccinated. The link also used a narrow filter that hid WHO's other HPV rows. (Second accuracy pass; WHO immunisation data API rows for BIH with the broad HPV filter, read as raw JSON) · Reworded to last-dose programme coverage of 9% of the girls targeted in 2024; WHO's coverage by age 15 added (7% first dose, 5% last dose, girls, 2024); link changed to the broad filter.
  • 2026-11-16 · Bosnia and Herzegovina country page: The registry card stated as fact that "there is no single national cancer registry". No source read says so. (Second accuracy pass; the Federation's 2021 health report, Republika Srpska's 2024 report and the European Network of Cancer Registries member list, read as raw text) · Reworded: cancer is registered entity by entity, and OnCo found no single national registry.
  • 2026-11-16 · Bosnia and Herzegovina country page: The clinical trials card said the medicines agency can inspect trial sites. (Second accuracy pass; the Agency's clinical trials page, read as raw HTML) · Reworded to what the page says: a trial's proposer may ask the Agency to check a trial where it is run, and the Agency may stop or ban a trial it is supervising.
  • 2026-11-16 · Bosnia and Herzegovina country page: The shortages card dated earlier Sarajevo cytostatics notices 26 October and 2 November 2026 and called the Federation insurer's list to 6 November the latest; it gave ifosfamide without saying the notice names the brand; and Republika Srpska's medical aids were given as including "breast prostheses". (Second accuracy pass; the Sarajevo clinical centre's notice list, the Federation insurer's medicines page and the Republika Srpska fund's guide, read as raw text) · Notices now dated 2, 9 and 16 November 2026; a supply interruption list dated 13 November 2026 is noted; ifosfamide is marked as listed under the brand Holoksan; the aids read "wigs, bras and prostheses".
  • 2026-11-16 · Chad country page: The gap card said the directory's "public list of countries ... holds 155 countries" and that Chad is not among them (Second accuracy pass; IAEA DIRAC public equipment feed read as raw JSON on 16 November 2026) · The gap card says the directory's public data, read on 16 November 2026, holds 8,627 entries from 157 countries and territories and lists no centre in Chad, by name or country code; it says this is an absence from a directory, not proof that Chad has no radiotherapy, and keeps WHO's count of no machines for 2019
  • 2026-11-16 · Zambia country page: The gap card said the directory's "country table is filled by script from a data service that was not read"; the page's only machine count was the Ministry's 2022 plan (one linear accelerator, two cobalt-60 units, two brachytherapy units) (Second accuracy pass; IAEA DIRAC public equipment feed read as raw JSON on 16 November 2026) · The treatment card and the gap card state what the directory lists: one centre, Cancer Diseases Hospital in Lusaka, with four linear accelerators, no cobalt-60 unit and two high dose rate iridium-192 brachytherapy units, entry last updated 2026. The Ministry's 2022 count is kept beside it and the difference is stated
